It is based on LibFuzzer and provides native Python fuzzing capabilities with coverage instrumentation.\n\n## Installation\n\n```bash\npip install atheris\n```\n\nFor better coverage, also install the coverage instrumentation:\n\n```bash\npip install atheris[libfuzzer]\n```\n\n## Basic Fuzz Driver Structure\n\nA minimal Atheris fuzz driver follows this pattern:\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\n# Import the library under test\n# import target_library\n\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes):\n    \"\"\"Fuzz target function - receives random bytes as input.\"\"\"\n    # Convert bytes to appropriate format for the target\n    try:\n        # Call the function under test with the fuzzed input\n        # target_library.function(data)\n        pass\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):\n        # Expected exceptions that indicate invalid input, not bugs\n        pass\n\ndef main():\n    # Setup Atheris with coverage instrumentation\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    # Run the fuzzer\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n```\n\n## Using FuzzedDataProvider\n\nFor more structured fuzzing inputs, use `FuzzedDataProvider`:\n\n```python\nimport atheris\n\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Generate different data types\n    integer = fdp.ConsumeInt(4)           # 4-byte integer\n    string = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(100)  # Unicode string\n    boolean = fdp.ConsumeBool()            # Boolean\n    float_val = fdp.ConsumeFloat()         # Float\n    remaining = fdp.ConsumeBytes(fdp.remaining_bytes())  # Rest of data\n\n    # Use these values to call target functions\n```\n\n### FuzzedDataProvider Methods\n\n| Method | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `ConsumeBytes(n)` | Consume up to n bytes |\n| `ConsumeInt(n)` | Consume n-byte integer |\n| `ConsumeIntInRange(min, max)` | Integer in range |\n| `ConsumeFloat()` | Consume a float |\n| `ConsumeFloatInRange(min, max)` | Float in range |\n| `ConsumeBool()` | Consume a boolean |\n| `ConsumeString(n)` | Consume n-char string |\n| `ConsumeUnicode(n)` | Consume n Unicode chars |\n| `ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(n)` | Unicode without surrogates |\n| `remaining_bytes()` | Bytes left in buffer |\n\n## Running the Fuzzer\n\n### Basic Run\n\n```bash\npython fuzz.py\n```\n\n### Time-Limited Run\n\n```bash\n# Run for 10 seconds\ntimeout 10 python fuzz.py 2>&1 | tee fuzz.log\n# Or using atheris built-in:\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10\n```\n\n### With Corpus Directory\n\n```bash\nmkdir corpus\npython fuzz.py corpus/\n```\n\n### Common Command-Line Options (LibFuzzer-style)\n\n- `-max_total_time=N`: Run for N seconds maximum\n- `-max_len=N`: Maximum input length\n- `-timeout=N`: Timeout per test case\n- `-jobs=N`: Number of parallel jobs\n- `-workers=N`: Number of worker processes\n- `-dict=file`: Use dictionary file\n\n## Exception Handling Best Practices\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes):\n    try:\n        # Target code\n        pass\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, IndexError) as e:\n        # Expected exceptions from invalid input - ignore\n        pass\n    except MemoryError:\n        # Resource exhaustion - ignore\n        pass\n    # Don't catch: crashes, assertion errors, or unexpected exceptions\n```\n\n## Example: Fuzzing a JSON Parser\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\nwith atheris.instrument_imports():\n    import json\n\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes):\n    try:\n        json.loads(data)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):\n        pass\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n## Instrumenting Imports\n\nUse `instrument_imports()` context manager for coverage:\n\n```python\nwith atheris.instrument_imports():\n    import target_library\n    from target_library import specific_function\n```\n\nThis ensures the fuzzer tracks coverage inside the imported modules.\n\n## Output and Logs\n\nAtheris outputs statistics during fuzzing:\n- `#N`: Number of test cases run\n- `cov`: Coverage edges discovered\n- `ft`: Feature count\n- `corp`: Corpus size\n\nCrashes are saved as `crash-<hash>` files in the current directory.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":337,"cache_read_input_tokens":19315,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":337,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6a6106f5-8fa3-4257-954e-c2b27f0b331d","uuid":"129f016c-72d2-4c06-bd6c-eba774d20211"}

Python Virtual Environment Setup\n\n## Overview\n\nPython virtual environments isolate project dependencies, ensuring each project has its own package versions without conflicts.\n\n## Creating a Virtual Environment\n\n### Using venv (Python 3.3+)\n\n```bash\n# Create venv in current directory\npython3 -m venv .venv\n\n# Create venv with specific Python version\npython3.10 -m venv .venv\n```\n\n### Common .venv Location\n\nBy convention, virtual environments are often placed in the project root:\n```\n/path/to/project/.venv/\n```\n\n## Activating the Virtual Environment\n\n```bash\n# Linux/macOS\nsource .venv/bin/activate\n\n# Windows (Command Prompt)\n.venv\\Scripts\\activate.bat\n\n# Windows (PowerShell)\n.venv\\Scripts\\Activate.ps1\n```\n\n## Installing Dependencies\n\n### From requirements.txt\n\n```bash\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### From pyproject.toml (Modern Python)\n\n```bash\n# Install the package in editable mode with dependencies\npip install -e .\n\n# Install with optional dependencies\npip install -e \".[dev]\"\npip install -e \".[test]\"\n```\n\n### From setup.py\n\n```bash\npip install -e .\n```\n\n## Common Project Configuration Files\n\n### requirements.txt\nPlain text file with package specifications:\n```\nnumpy>=1.20.0\npandas==2.0.0\nrequests\n```\n\n### pyproject.toml\nModern Python project configuration:\n```toml\n[project]\ndependencies = [\n    \"numpy>=1.20\",\n    \"pandas\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\"]\n```\n\n### setup.py\nLegacy setup script:\n```python\nfrom setuptools import setup\nsetup(\n    install_requires=[\"numpy\", \"pandas\"],\n    extras_require={\"dev\": [\"pytest\"]},\n)\n```\n\n### setup.cfg\nConfiguration-based setup:\n```ini\n[options]\ninstall_requires =\n    numpy\n    pandas\n```\n\n## Running Commands in Virtual Environment\n\n### With Activation\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npython script.py\npip install package\ndeactivate\n```\n\n### Without Activation (Direct Path)\n\n```bash\n.venv/bin/python script.py\n.venv/bin/pip install package\n```\n\n## Complete Setup Example\n\n```bash\n# Navigate to project\ncd /path/to/project\n\n# Create virtual environment\npython3 -m venv .venv\n\n# Install dependencies based on what's available\nif [ -f \"requirements.txt\" ]; then\n    .venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt\nfi\n\nif [ -f \"pyproject.toml\" ]; then\n    .venv/bin/pip install -e .\nfi\n\nif [ -f \"setup.py\" ]; then\n    .venv/bin/pip install -e .\nfi\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Missing pip\n\n```bash\npython3 -m ensurepip --upgrade\n```\n\n### Upgrade pip\n\n```bash\n.venv/bin/pip install --upgrade pip\n```\n\n### Clear and .venv\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1367,"cache_read_input_tokens":19652,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1367,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6a6106f5-8fa3-4257-954e-c2b27f0b331d","uuid":"000f3ae4-621c-4df0-9d2d-07d08a187e2b"}

Python Virtual Environment Setup\n\n## Overview\n\nPython virtual environments isolate project dependencies, ensuring each project has its own package versions without conflicts.\n\n## Creating a Virtual Environment\n\n### Using venv (Python 3.3+)\n\n```bash\n# Create venv in current directory\npython3 -m venv .venv\n\n# Create venv with specific Python version\npython3.10 -m venv .venv\n```\n\n### Common .venv Location\n\nBy convention, virtual environments are often placed in the project root:\n```\n/path/to/project/.venv/\n```\n\n## Activating the Virtual Environment\n\n```bash\n# Linux/macOS\nsource .venv/bin/activate\n\n# Windows (Command Prompt)\n.venv\\Scripts\\activate.bat\n\n# Windows (PowerShell)\n.venv\\Scripts\\Activate.ps1\n```\n\n## Installing Dependencies\n\n### From requirements.txt\n\n```bash\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### From pyproject.toml (Modern Python)\n\n```bash\n# Install the package in editable mode with dependencies\npip install -e .\n\n# Install with optional dependencies\npip install -e \".[dev]\"\npip install -e \".[test]\"\n```\n\n### From setup.py\n\n```bash\npip install -e .\n```\n\n## Common Project Configuration Files\n\n### requirements.txt\nPlain text file with package specifications:\n```\nnumpy>=1.20.0\npandas==2.0.0\nrequests\n```\n\n### pyproject.toml\nModern Python project configuration:\n```toml\n[project]\ndependencies = [\n    \"numpy>=1.20\",\n    \"pandas\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\"]\n```\n\n### setup.py\nLegacy setup script:\n```python\nfrom setuptools import setup\nsetup(\n    install_requires=[\"numpy\", \"pandas\"],\n    extras_require={\"dev\": [\"pytest\"]},\n)\n```\n\n### setup.cfg\nConfiguration-based setup:\n```ini\n[options]\ninstall_requires =\n    numpy\n    pandas\n```\n\n## Running Commands in Virtual Environment\n\n### With Activation\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npython script.py\npip install package\ndeactivate\n```\n\n### Without Activation (Direct Path)\n\n```bash\n.venv/bin/python script.py\n.venv/bin/pip install package\n```\n\n## Complete Setup 283→            )\n   284→\n   285→        has_time = has_space_divider or has_t_divider\n   286→        has_tz = False\n   287→\n   288→        # date formats (ISO 8601 and others) to test against\n   289→        # NOTE: YYYYMM is omitted to avoid confusion with YYMMDD (no longer part of ISO 8601, but is still often used)\n   290→        formats = [\n   291→            \"YYYY-MM-DD\",\n   292→            \"YYYY-M-DD\",\n   293→            \"YYYY-M-D\",\n   294→            \"YYYY/MM/DD\",\n   295→            \"YYYY/M/DD\",\n   296→            \"YYYY/M/D\",\n   297→            \"YYYY.MM.DD\",\n ParserError(\n   324→                    \"Invalid time component provided. \"\n   325→                    \"Please specify a format or provide a valid time component in the basic or extended ISO 8601 time format.\"\n   326→                )\n   327→\n   328→            (\n   329→                hours,\n   330→                minutes,\n   331→                seconds,\n   332→                subseconds_sep,\n   333→                subseconds,\n   334→            ) = time_components.groups()\n   335→\n   336→            has_tz = len(time_parts) == 2\n   337→            has_minutes = minutes is not None\n   338→            has_seconds = seconds is not None\n   339→            has_subseconds = subseconds is not None\n   340→\n   341→            is_basic_time_format = \":\" not in 371→        return self._parse_multiformat(datetime_string, formats)\n   372→\n   373→    def parse(\n   374→        self,\n   375→        datetime_string: str,\n   376→        fmt: Union[List[str], str],\n   377→        normalize_whitespace: bool = False,\n   378→    ) -> datetime:\n   379→        \"\"\"\n   380→        Parses a datetime string using a specified format.\n   381→\n   382→        :param datetime_string: The datetime string to parse.\n   383→        :param fmt: The format string or list of format strings to use for parsing.\n   384→        :param normalize_whitespace: Whether to normalize whitespace in the datetime string (default is False).\n   385→        :type datetime_string: str\n   386→        :type fmt: Union[List[str], str]\n   387→        :type normalize_whitespace: bool\n   388→        :returns: The parsed datetime object.\n   389→        :rtype: datetime\n   390→        :raises ParserMatchError: If the datetime string does not match the specified format.\n   391→\n   392→        Usage::\n   393→\n   394→        >>> import arrow.parser\n   395→        >>> arrow.parser.DateTimeParser().parse('2021-10-12 14:30:00', 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm:ss')\n   396→        datetime.datetime(2021, 10, 12, 14, 30)\n   397→\n   398→\n   399→        \"\"\"\n   400→        if normalize_whitespace:\n   401→            datetime_string = re.sub(r\"\\s+\", \" \", datetime_string)\n   402→\n   403→        if isinstance(fmt, list):\n   404→            return self._parse_multiformat(datetime_string, fmt)\n   405→\n   406→        try:\n   407→            fmt_tokens: List[_FORMAT_TYPE]\n   408→            fmt_pattern_re: Pattern[str]\n   409→            fmt_tokens, fmt_pattern_re = self._generate_pattern_re(fmt)\n   410→        except re.error as e:\n   411→            raise ParserMatchError(\n   412→                f\"Failed to generate regular expression pattern: {e}.\"\n   413→            )\n   414→\n   415→        match = fmt_pattern_re.search(datetime_string)\n   416→\n   417→        if match is None:\n   418→            raise ParserMatchError(\n   419→                f\"Failed to match {fmt!r} when parsing {datetime_string!r}.\"\n   420→            )\n   421→\n   422→        parts: _Parts = {}\n   423→        for token in fmt_tokens:\n   424→            value: Union[Tuple[str, str, str], str]\n   425→ value, parts)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n   438→\n   439→        return self._build_datetime(parts)\n   440→\n   441→    def _generate_pattern_re(self, fmt: str) -> Tuple[List[_FORMAT_TYPE], Pattern[str]]:\n   442→        \"\"\"\n   443→        Generates a regular expression pattern from a format string.\n   444→\n   445→        :param fmt: The format string to convert into a regular expression pattern.\n   446→        :type fmt: str\n   447→        :returns: A tuple containing a list of format tokens and the corresponding regular expression pattern.\n   448→        :rtype: Tuple[List[_FORMAT_TYPE], Pattern[str]]\n   449→        :raises ParserError: If an unrecognized token is encountered in the format string.\n   450→        \"\"\"\n   451→        # fmt is a string of tokens like 'YYYY-MM-DD'\n   452→        # we construct a new string by replacing each\n   453→        # token by its pattern:\n   454→        # 'YYYY-MM-DD' -> '(?P<YYYY>\\d{4})-(?P<MM>\\d{2})-(?P<DD>\\d{2})'\n   455→        tokens: List[_FORMAT_TYPE] = []\n   456→        offset = 0\n   457→\n   458→        # Escape all special RegEx chars\n   459→        escaped_fmt = re.escape(fmt)\n   460→\n   461→        # Extract the bracketed expressions to be reinserted later.\n   462→        escaped_fmt = re.sub(self._ESCAPE_RE, \"#\", escaped_fmt)\n   463→\n   464→        # Any number of S is the same as one.\n   465→        # TODO: allow users to specify the number of digits to parse\n   466→        escaped_fmt = re.sub(r\"S+\", \"S\", escaped_fmt)\n   467→\n   468→        escaped_data = re.findall(self._ESCAPE_RE, fmt)\n   469→\n   470→        fmt_pattern = escaped_fmt\n   471→\n   472→        for m in self._FORMAT_RE.finditer(escaped_fmt):\n   473→            token: _FORMAT_TYPE = cast(_FORMAT_TYPE, m.group(0))\n   474→            try:\n   475→                input_re = self._input_re_map[token]\n   476→            except KeyError:\n   477→                raise ParserError(f\"Unrecognized token {token!r}.\")\n   478→            input_pattern = f\"(?P<{token}>{input_re.pattern})\"\n   479→            tokens.append(token)\n   480→            # a pattern doesn't have the same length as the token\n   481→            # it replaces! We keep the difference in the offset variable.\n   482→            # This works because the string is scanned left-to-right and matches\n   483→            # are returned in the order found by finditer.\n   484→            fmt_pattern = (\n   485→                fmt_pattern[: m.start() + offset]\n   486→                + input_pattern\n   487→                + fmt_pattern[m.end() + offset :]\n   488→            )\n   489→            offset += len(input_pattern) - (m.end() - m.start())\n   490→\n   491→        final_fmt_pattern = \"\"\n   492→        split_fmt = fmt_pattern.split(r\"\\#\")\n   493→\n   494→        # Due to the way Python splits, 'split_fmt' will always be longer\n   495→        for i in range(len(split_fmt)):\n   496→            final_fmt_pattern += split_fmt[i]\n   497→            if i < len(escaped_data):\n   498→                final_fmt_pattern += escaped_data[i][1:-1]\n   499→\n   500→        # Wrap final_fmt_pattern in a custom word boundary to strictly\n   501→        # match the formatting pattern and filter out date and time formats\n   502→        # that include junk such as: blah1998-09-12 blah, blah 1998-09-12blah,\n   503→        # blah1998-09-12blah. The custom word boundary matches every character\n   504→        # that is not a whitespace character to allow for searching for a date\n   505→        # and time string in a natural language sentence. Therefore, searching\n   506→        # for a string of the form YYYY-MM-DD in \"blah 1998-09-12 blah\" will\n   507→        # work properly.\n   508→        # Certain punctuation before or after the target pattern such as\n   509→        # \"1998-09-12,\" is permitted. For the full list of valid punctuation,\n   510→        # see the documentation.\n   511→\n   512→        starting_word_boundary = (\n   513→            r\"(?<!\\S\\S)\"  # Don't have two consecutive non-whitespace characters. This ensures that we allow cases\n   514→            # like .11.25.2019 but not 1.11.25.2019 (for pattern MM.DD.YYYY)\n   515→            r\"(?<![^\\,\\.\\;\\:\\?\\!\\\"\\'\\`\\[\\]\\{\\}\\(\\)<>\\s])\"  # This is the list of punctuation that is ok before the\n   516→            # pattern (i.e. \"It can't not be these characters before the pattern\")\n   517→            r\"(\\b|^)\"\n   518→            # The \\b is to block cases like 1201912 but allow 201912 for pattern YYYYMM. The ^ was necessary to allow a\n   519→            # negative number through i.e. before epoch numbers\n   520→        )\n   521→        ending_word_boundary = (\n   522→            r\"(?=[\\,\\.\\;\\:\\?\\!\\\"\\'\\`\\[\\]\\{\\}\\(\\)\\<\\>]?\"  # Positive lookahead stating that these punctuation marks\n   523→            # can appear after the pattern at most 1 time\n   524→            r\"(?!\\S))\"  # Don't allow any non-whitespace character after the punctuation\n   525→        )\n   526→        bounded_fmt_pattern = r\"{}{}{}\".format(\n   527→            starting_word_boundary, final_fmt_pattern, ending_word_boundary\n   528→        )\n   529→\n   530→        return tokens, re.compile(bounded_fmt_pattern, flags=re.IGNORECASE)\n   531→\n   532→    @overload\n   533→    def _parse_token(\n   534→        self,\n   535→        token: Literal[\n   536→            \"YYYY\",\n   537→            \"YY\",\n   538→            \"MM\",\n   539→            \"M\",\n   540→            \"DDDD\",\n   541→            \"DDD\",\n   542→            \"DD\",\n   543→            \"D\",\n   544→            \"Do\",\n   545→            \"HH\",\n   546→            \"hh\",\n   547→            \"h\",\n   548→            \"H\",\n   549→            \"mm\",\n   550→            \"m\",\n   551→            \"ss\",\n   552→            \"s\",\n   553→            \"x\",\n   554→        ],\n   555→        value: Union[str, bytes, SupportsInt, bytearray],\n   556→        parts: _Parts,\n   557→    ) -> None: ...  # pragma: no cover\n   582→\n   583→    @overload\n   584→    def _parse_token(\n   585→        self,\n   586→        token: Literal[\"W\"],\n   587→        value: Tuple[_WEEKDATE_ELEMENT, _WEEKDATE_ELEMENT, Optional[_WEEKDATE_ELEMENT]],\n   588→        parts: _Parts,\n   589→    ) -> None: ...  # pragma: no cover\n   590→\n   591→    def _parse_token(\n   592→        self,\n   593→        token: Any,\n   594→        value: Any,\n   595→        parts: _Parts,\n   596→    ) -> None:\n   597→        \"\"\"\n   598→        Parse a token and its value, and update the `_Parts` dictionary with the parsed values.\n   599→\n   600→        The function supports several tokens, including \"YYYY\", \"YY\", \"MMMM\", \"MMM\", \"MM\", \"M\", \"DDDD\", \"DDD\", \"DD\", \"D\", \"Do\", \"dddd\", \"ddd\", \"HH\", \"H\", \"mm\", \"m\", \"ss\", \"s\", \"S\", \"X\", \"x\", \"ZZZ\", \"ZZ\", \"Z\", \"a\", \"A\", and \"W\". Each token is matched and the corresponding value is parsed and added to the `_Parts` dictionary.\n   601→\n   602→        :param token: The token to parse.\n   603→        :type token: Any\n   604→        :param value: The value of the token.\n   605→        :type value: Any\n   606→        :param parts: A dictionary to update with the parsed values.\n   607→        :type parts: _Parts\n   608→        :raises ParserMatchError: If the hour token value is not between 0 and 12 inclusive for tokens \"a\" or \"A\".\n   609→\n   610→        \"\"\"\n   611→        if token == \"YYYY\":\n   612→            parts[\"year\"] = int(value)\n   613→\n   614→        elif token == \"YY\":\n   615→            value = int(value)\n   616→            parts[\"year\"] = 1900 + value if value > 68 else 2000 + value\n   617→\n   618→        elif token in [\"MMMM\", \"MMM\"]:\n   619→            # FIXME: month_number() is nullable\n   620→            parts[\"month\"] = self.locale.month_number(value.lower())  # type: ignore[typeddict-item]\n   621→\n   622→        elif token in [\"MM\", \"M\"]:\n   623→            parts[\"month\"] = int(value)\n   624→\n   625→        elif token in [\"DDDD\", \"DDD\"]:\n   626→            parts[\"day_of_year\"] = int(value)\n   627→\n   628→        elif token in [\"DD\", \"D\"]:\n   629→            parts[\"day\"] = int(value)\n   630→\n   631→        elif token == \"Do\":\n   632→            parts[\"day\"] = int(value)\n   633→\n   634→        elif token == \"dddd\":\n   635→            # locale day names are 1-indexed\n   636→            day_of_week = [x.lower() for x in self.locale.day_names].index(\n   637→                value.lower()\n   638→            )\n   639→            parts[\"day_of_week\"] = day_of_week - 1\n   640→\n   641→        elif token == \"ddd\":\n   642→            # locale day abbreviations are 1-indexed\n   643→            day_of_week = [x.lower() for x in self.locale.day_abbreviations].index(\n   644→                value.lower()\n   645→            )\n   646→            parts[\"day_of_week\"] = day_of_week - 1\n   647→\n   648→        elif token.upper() in [\"HH\", \"H\"]:\n   649→            parts[\"hour\"] = int(value)\n   650→\n   651→        elif token in [\"mm\", \"m\"]:\n   652→            parts[\"minute\"] = int(value)\n   653→\n   654→        elif token in [\"ss\", \"s\"]:\n   655→            parts[\"second\"] = int(value)\n   656→\n   657→        elif token == \"S\":\n   658→            # We have the *most significant* digits of an arbitrary-precision integer.\n   659→            # We want the six most significant digits as an integer, rounded.\n   660→            # IDEA: add nanosecond support somehow? Need datetime support for it first.\n   661→            value = value.ljust(7, \"0\")\n   662→\n   663→            # floating-point (IEEE-754) defaults to half-to-even rounding\n   664→            seventh_digit = int(value[6])\n   665→            if seventh_digit == 5:\n   666→                rounding = int(value[5]) % 2\n   667→            elif seventh_digit > 5:\n   668→                rounding = 1\n   669→            else:\n   670→                rounding = 0\n   671→\n   672→            parts[\"microsecond\"] = int(value[:6]) + rounding\n   673→\n   674→        elif token == \"X\":\n   675→            parts[\"timestamp\"] = float(value)\n   676→\n   677→        elif token == \"x\":\n   678→            parts[\"expanded_timestamp\"] = int(value)\n   679→\n   680→        elif token in [\"ZZZ\", \"ZZ\", \"Z\"]:\n   681→            parts[\"tzinfo\"] = TzinfoParser.parse(value)\n   682→\n   683→        elif token in [\"a\", \"A\"]:\n   684→            if value in (self.locale.meridians[\"am\"], self.locale.meridians[\"AM\"]):\n   685→                parts[\"am_pm\"] = \"am\"\n   686→                if \"hour\" in parts and not 0 <= parts[\"hour\"] <= 12:\n   687→                    raise ParserMatchError(\n   688→                        f\"Hour token value must be between 0 and 12 inclusive for token {token!r}.\"\n   689→                    )\n   690→            elif value in (self.locale.meridians[\"pm\"], self.locale.meridians[\"PM\"]):\n   691→                parts[\"am_pm\"] = \"pm\"\n   692→        elif token == \"W\":\n   693→            parts[\"weekdate\"] = value\n   694→\n   695→    @staticmethod\n   696→    def _build_datetime(parts: _Parts) -> datetime:\n   697→        \"\"\"\n   698→        Build a datetime object from a    :returns: The parsed datetime object.\n        :rtype: datetime\n        :raises ParserMatchError: If the datetime string does not match the specified format.\n\n        Usage::\n\n        >>> Tuple[List[_FORMAT_TYPE], Pattern[str]]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Generates a regular expression pattern from a format string.\n\n        :param fmt: The format string to convert into a regular expression pattern.\n        :type fmt: str\n        :returns: A tuple containing a list of format tokens and the corresponding regular expression pattern.\n        :rtype: Tuple[List[_FORMAT_TYPE], Pattern[str]]\n        :raises ParserError: If an unrecognized token is encountered in the format string.\n        \"\"\"\n        # fmt is a string of tokens like 'YYYY-MM-DD'\n        # we construct a new string by replacing each\n        # token by its pattern:\n        # 'YYYY-MM-DD' -> '(?P<YYYY>\\d{4})-(?P<MM>\\d{2})-(?P<DD>\\d{2})'\n        tokens: List[_FORMAT_TYPE] = []\n        offset = 0\n\n        # Escape all special RegEx chars\n        escaped_fmt = re.escape(fmt)\n\n        # Extract the bracketed expressions to be reinserted later.\n        escaped_fmt = re.sub(self._ESCAPE_RE, \"#\", escaped_fmt)\n\n        # Any number of S is the same as one.\n        # TODO: allow users to specify the number of digits to parse\n        escaped_fmt = re.sub(r\"S+\", \"S\", escaped_fmt)\n\n        escaped_data = re.findall(self._ESCAPE_RE, fmt)\n\n        fmt_pattern = escaped_fmt\n\n        for m in self._FORMAT_RE.finditer(escaped_fmt):\n            token: _FORMAT_TYPE = cast(_FORMAT_TYPE, m.group(0))\n            try:\n                input_re = self._input_re_map[token]\n            except KeyError:\n                raise ParserError(f\"Unrecognized token {token!r}.\")\n            input_pattern = f\"(?P<{token}>{input_re.pattern})\"\n            tokens.append(token)\n            # a pattern doesn't have the same length as the token\n            # it replaces! We keep the difference in the offset variable.\n            # This works because the string is scanned left-to-right and matches\n            # are returned in the order found by finditer.\n            fmt_pattern = (\n                fmt_pattern[: m.start() + offset]\n                + input_pattern\n                + fmt_pattern[m.end() + offset :]\n            )\n            offset += len(input_pattern) - (m.end() - m.start())\n\n        final_fmt_pattern = \"\"\n        split_fmt = fmt_pattern.split(r\"\\#\")\n\n        # Due to the way Python splits, 'split_fmt' will always be longer\n        for i in range(len(split_fmt)):\n            final_fmt_pattern += split_fmt[i]\n            if i < len(escaped_data):\n                final_fmt_pattern += escaped_data[i][1:-1]\n\n        # Wrap final_fmt_pattern in a custom word boundary to strictly\n        # match the formatting pattern and filter out date and time formats\n        # that include junk such as: blah1998-09-12 blah, blah 1998-09-12blah,\n        # blah1998-09-12blah. The custom word boundary matches every character\n        # that is not a whitespace character to allow for searching for a date\n        # and time string in a natural language sentence. Therefore, searching\n        # for a string of the form YYYY-MM-DD in \"blah 1998-09-12 blah\" will\n        # work properly.\n        # Certain punctuation before or after the target pattern such as\n        # \"1998-09-12,\" is permitted. For the full list of valid punctuation,\n        # see the documentation.\n\n        starting_word_boundary = (\n            r\"(?<!\\S\\S)\"  # Don't have two consecutive non-whitespace characters. This ensures that we allow cases\n            # like .11.25.2019 but not 1.11.25.2019 (for pattern MM.DD.YYYY)\n            r\"(?<![^\\,\\.\\;\\:\\?\\!\\\"\\'\\`\\[\\]\\{\\}\\(\\)<>\\s])\"  # This is the list of punctuation that is ok before the\n            # pattern (i.e. \"It can't not be these characters before the pattern\")\n            r\"(\\b|^)\"\n            # The \\b is to block cases like 1201912 but allow 201912 for pattern YYYYMM. The ^ was necessary to allow a\n            # negative number through i.e. before epoch numbers\n        )\n        ending_word_boundary = (\n            r\"(?=[\\,\\.\\;\\:\\?\\!\\\"\\'\\`\\[\\]\\{\\}\\(\\)\\<\\>]?\"  # Positive lookahead stating that these punctuation marks\n            # can appear after the pattern at most 1 time\n            r\"(?!\\S))\"  # Don't allow any non-whitespace character after the punctuation\n        )\n        bounded_fmt_pattern = r\"{}{}{}\".format(\n            starting_word_boundary, final_fmt_pattern, ending_word_boundary\n        )\n\n        return tokens, re.compile(bounded_fmt_pattern, flags=re.IGNORECASE)\n\n    @overload\n    def _parse_token(\n        self,\n        token: Literal[\n            \"YYYY\",\n            \"YY\",\n            \"MM\",\n            \"M\",\n            \"DDDD\",\n            \"DDD\",\n            \"DD\",\n            \"D\",\n            \"Do\",\n            \"HH\",\n            \"hh\",\n            \"h\",\n            \"H\",\n            \"mm\",\n            \"m\",\n            \"ss\",\n            \"s\",\n            \"x\",\n        ],\n        value: Union[str, bytes, SupportsInt, bytearray],\n        parts: _Parts,\n    ) -> None: ...  #
